Cassian refuse to fill the missing section of his account with a consistent story?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. He wants to transfer responsibility for every action to Mara.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. He wants responsibility assessed against actual evidence rather than an invented completion of his memory.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. He has decided that no surviving record should be examined.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. Inside, the mechanism looked smaller than she remembered.","\"They have improved its importance,\" she whispered.","Cassian looked at the foam lining. \"Considerably.\"","An independent examiner checked her identification, the court's direction, and the custody record before opening the case. Mara had expected a lecture about responsibility. Instead, the examiner asked which surface could safely be touched without stressing the old repair. It was such a useful question that she liked him immediately.","The mechanism, strip, and associated documents had been separately recorded. Relevant data had been copied and certified; the originals required for further examination would remain protected. The flower itself could return to Mara as its recorded conservation custodian. She would preserve it, document any later intervention, and make it available when directed. Its return did not settle every ownership question. It did mean that the original object, rather than a newly convenient substitute, could leave the room with her.","She read the conditions and signed. The receipt seemed absurdly ordinary for something that had occupied so much of her life.","The examiner had one more observation. Among the later fittings was a tiny directional indicator. Their recorded inspection confirmed the response Mara had seen aboard the ship: one petal had turned when the mechanism was brought close to a matched active cable from the preserved equipment. The movement occurred only at short range. It had not revealed a person's memories or the location of distant stations. It was a small response from a small component, useful if one understood what was nearby.","\"My mother changed that petal,\" Mara said.","\"The repair register agrees. We have documented its condition. Don't force it against resistance.\"","She almost laughed. It was the first instruction she had ever received about the flower that sounded like advice for an object rather than a demand about a conspiracy.","The examiner placed it in her palm. Its weight was familiar at once. She closed her fingers around it and discovered she had been holding her breath.","Cassian remained quiet until they were outside. Then he held out his hand, and she gave him the chain.","He fastened it at her throat with the same care he had used on the collar before court. The flower rested against the fabric, neither an emblem of a family she had never claimed nor a pardon for everything she had done. It was her mother's work, the city's disputed property, and her responsibility for the moment. She could carry all three histories without choosing the easiest one.","\"Better?\" he asked.","She touched the folded petal. \"Ask me after I've checked the clasp twice.\"","He kissed her forehead. \"A professional standard I understand.\"","They took the long route to Mercy House, stopping at a shop for pencils and the bread Elena preferred. Cassian also bought an unnecessary little paper windmill from a child outside the bakery. He claimed it was for Admiral. Mara suspected that the tall, solemn man had simply been unable to refuse a saleswoman who reached his knee.","Elena examined the flower from her chair beside the window. Mara told her what the examiner had observed. Her mother held the mechanism under the lamp, tracing the added fitting without moving it.","\"A checker,\" she said after a while. \"I used something like this near the equipment. I wanted to see which part was active without taking the cover off.\"","\"Do you remember making it?\"","Elena's eyes stayed on the petal. \"Pieces. Not the whole morning.\"","Mara sat beside her. \"Then pieces are enough for today.\"","Her mother gave her a look. \"I was about to say that myself. You are becoming tiresomely sensible.\"","They laughed together. Noor, passing the door, stopped long enough to confirm that lunch was on its way and sensible people were allowed pudding.","Before the visit, Elena spread the copied pages on her blanket and selected two. She left the others facedown.","\"These I can identify,\" she said. \"The rest I would be describing because somebody has put my name beside them. I have had quite enough of that arrangement.\"","Mara sat on the floor beside the chair. Her mother ran a finger over a familiar pencil correction and smiled at something she did not explain.","\"You don't need to do this for me,\" Mara said.","\"I am doing some of it for myself. I objected then. I'd like the objection to arrive somewhere it can't be put in the wrong drawer quite so easily.\"","Mara took her hand. There was room in the act for fear, stubbornness, and the old pride in useful work. She did not need to turn it into a sacrifice.","A court-appointed recorder came in the afternoon. Elena had agreed to a short private statement about the work she could identify. Noor remained available, and the questioning stopped when Elena tired. She recognized the hinge repair, the margin flower, and the recruitment draft in which she had objected to combining later use of recordings with payment. She described what she remembered directly and declined to supply what she did not.","Mara waited outside. There were few things she wanted more than to hear every word, and none that justified making the statement hers to manage. Cassian found her pacing between the stair and the old clock.","\"Eight lengths,\" he said.","\"Have you been counting?\"","\"I considered asking the clock to keep a record. It seems unreliable.\"","\"I repaired it.\"","\"Then I withdraw the criticism immediately.\"","She caught his sleeve and kept him beside her until the door opened.","The recorder sealed the statement and explained the next stage through counsel. Certified copies would join the protected court record with the hospital transfers and Cassian's examination report. Independent experts would compare the material. Elena's words would not be stretched into a complete explanation merely because her daughter had waited years to hear them.","When Mara returned to the room, Elena was drawing the windmill. Cassian had left it in a glass beside the bed. Its red paper blades looked cheerful against the blue blanket.","\"He bought this for the dog,\" Mara said.","\"A highly cultured dog.\"","\"You are both impossible.\"","Elena looked up. \"Does he make you happy?\"","The question arrived without warning. Mara glanced towards the door, where Cassian was speaking quietly with Noor. She thought of the formal pass left beside the breakfast plate, the ordinary biscuits, the way he had waited while she chose the door.","\"Yes,\" she said.","Her mother's fingers settled over hers. \"Then let that be one of the things you keep.\"","That evening, they attempted the promised meal without documents. Mrs. Wren had removed the telephone from the dining room with an authority no court had yet matched. Admiral was shut out after making an unfounded claim to a roast chicken. Mara wore the green dress again because she had decided difficult days did not deserve exclusive ownership of it.","Cassian stood when she entered. She waited for the first sentence to fail and was rewarded.","\"You enjoy that,\" he said.","\"A modest compensation for the number of times you make me lose mine.\"","He drew out her chair. There was no staff watching the table, no photographer framing their hands, and no hidden objective behind the meal. For the first half hour, they managed a conversation about a neighbor's terrible singing and whether the theater queen had forgiven Mara for borrowing her coat.","Then she asked what he wanted to do after the inquiry.","He put down his glass. \"Work that doesn't require me to pretend a question has gone away because it is inconvenient. A little sleep. A home where you don't leave as though you are still waiting to be allowed to stay.\"","Mara looked at him for a long moment. The house key he had given her after the storm lay beside her ordinary keys now. She had been using it and still calling the place his house.","\"My tools take up a great deal of room,\" she said.","\"I have noticed.\"","\"My mother will visit. She will criticize your lamps.\"","\"I look forward to an expert opinion.\"","\"And I intend to keep my own workshop.\"","\"I should be alarmed if you didn't.\"","She smiled then, slowly. \"In that case, I think I can stay.\"","He reached across the table. She took his hand, and for a while they let the future be a place with room for both of them.","The knock came after pudding. Mrs. Wren opened the door before either could rise. June stood outside, soaked by rain, holding a phone rather than a folder.","The city had approved emergency protection for its communication infrastructure. A foundation-led committee would receive temporary access to connected clinical stations and public announcement systems.","The first implementation would begin before dawn."],"questions":[{"type":"scope","stem":"What does the flower's return to Mara mean at this stage?","options":["She may now alter the original without recording the work.","The associated class=\"question\" id=\"q-70-1\" data-question=\"69,0\"><p class=\"meta\">1. scope</p><p><strong>What does the flower&#x27;s return to Mara mean at this stage?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. She may now alter the original without recording the work.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. The associated documents are no longer protected.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. She becomes its recorded conservation custodian under conditions preserving access and later examination.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. He had gone to a temporary verification center because his employment account said he would lose access to payment if he failed to attend. Now the staff said he had to complete the procedure before he could leave.","The message ended with a photograph of a blue chair.","Noor put down the towel. \"That isn't the workplace fatigue monitor he agreed to.\"","Mara was already reaching for her coat.","The center occupied an old tram office near the water. A banner over its entrance promised continuity, security, and the protection of personal identity. The queue outside consisted of wet, tired people holding notices on their phones. Some had come because of work; others believed their benefits would be suspended. A few said they were simply curious. All were being directed through the same narrow doorway.","Cassian moved towards the guard, then stopped before his hand reached the empty place where his official pass had been. Mara saw the hesitation. She took his hand instead and carried it with her as they approached.","\"Our friend has asked to leave,\" Noor said. She gave Ewan's name and explained her clinical role.","The guard checked a screen. \"He's in process.\"","\"What process?\"","\"Completion.\"","Noor's expression grew very still. Mara had learned that this was a more serious warning than raised volume.","The guard would not let them through. Cassian asked for the supervisor's name. The man pointed to a call button that produced a recorded message about exceptional demand. Behind the glass door, Mara could see a reception desk, a corridor, and the edge of a blue chair beneath a bright lamp.","Then the silver flower moved against her coat.","She took it out. One petal turned towards a cable running inside the wall beside the entrance. The response was faint until she brought it close to the glass. It told her where an active part of the system lay, not what was happening to Ewan. That was enough to make waiting beside an unanswered button unbearable.","Ivo arrived in his van and looked at the building. \"They've joined a public terminal to a clinical station. The temporary wiring is still on the outside.\"","\"Can you turn it off?\" Cassian asked.","\"Not until I know what else it supplies. I would prefer not to discover a patient on the other end.\"","Mara looked along the wall. The building had once been a tram office, with a service counter opening into the covered passage behind it. She had bought old fittings there when the route closed. The counter had been boarded over. The small door beside it remained.","\"I can get inside,\" she said.","Noor began to object, then looked through the glass at the chairs. \"Get someone to open the public door. Don't touch equipment you don't understand.\"","\"A useful summary of my professional life.\"","Cassian followed her into the passage. Ivo stayed with Noor to identify the supply arrangements. Behind them, the queue had begun asking why a doctor could not enter. The guard was having trouble making the word completion sound reassuring.","The service door was old wood with new paint over a damaged frame. Mara worked at the loose fitting until it released. She slipped through, Cassian close behind, and found herself among boxes of printed appointment cards. The corridor beyond smelled of hot plastic and wet coats.","Ewan sat in the second cubicle. His hands were pressed flat against his knees. A technician was preparing a contact unit beside the chair while a woman in a gray jacket explained that withdrawal now would create an error in his employment record.","\"Then correct the record,\" Mara said.","The woman turned. \"You can't be here.\"","\"He asked for us.\"","\"The process has started.\"","Ewan looked up. His face changed at the sight of Noor's bag in Cassian's hand, then at Mara herself. \"I told them I didn't want the chair.\"","\"I heard you,\" Mara said.","The technician stepped back. He looked younger than the authority arranged around him. \"I haven't connected it yet.\"","\"Good,\" Cassian said. \"Keep it that way.\"","The woman reached for a wall phone. Mara did not wait for the argument to grow. She moved past the unused unit, opened the inner door, and called towards reception. \"A patient has asked for his doctor. Open the entrance.\"","The guard appeared at the corridor's end. For a second he looked ready to block her. Then the people outside began knocking on the glass. Not violently. In the same rhythm, as though somebody had given the queue a shared intention. Noor stood at the front, holding her identification where the camera could see it.","The guard opened the door.","Noor went straight to Ewan. She asked what had happened, whether he was uncomfortable, and whether anything had touched him. He answered. No contact unit had been attached. She told him they could discuss the employment notice somewhere quiet. The gray-jacketed woman said removal would be recorded as noncompliance.","\"Record his actual words,\" Noor replied. \"He does not consent to this procedure.\"","Ewan stood. His knees shook, but he walked beside her. The technician let them pass. He had put the unused unit down on its tray with the careful relief of someone who had been waiting for a reason to stop.","At the entrance, an elderly woman asked whether she could leave as well. Her phone had not yet shown the completion mark. The guard looked towards the supervisor. Cassian held the door open.","\"The pavement is public,\" he said.","She came out. Then a man followed, carrying a folder of benefit notices. Others remained, wanting more information. Mara did not order a crowd to trust her instead of the center. June arrived and began recording the questions being asked, the notices that had brought people there, and the answers staff were willing to give.","A woman came out carrying a folded employment notice. She had waited two hours and still did not know what her employer meant by verification. She asked Mara whether leaving would cost her the week's wages.","\"I can't promise what your employer will do,\" Mara said. \"Keep the notice. Write down when you arrived and what you asked. Ada can help you find advice without putting your name on a public screen.\"","The woman looked at the open street, then back at the blue chairs. She chose the street. Her friend followed, holding an umbrella over both of them.","June recorded the conditions on the displayed notice with the women's personal details out of view. A queue could look like agreement from a distance. From the doorway, it had been a collection of people trying to protect different ordinary parts of their lives.","The supervisor's phone rang. Her tone changed when she heard the caller. She demanded that the doors close and said emergency authority would be clarified shortly. The queue drew back from the entrance. Ivo photographed the terminal identifiers visible from outside and moved his van so Noor could take Ewan to Mercy House.","Mara stayed beside Cassian as two security vehicles turned into the street.","\"We should go,\" he said.","\"That sounds like excellent advice.\"","They left through the side passage before the vehicles reached the entrance. A shout followed. Cassian took her hand, and they ran between the old rails towards the covered market. Mara's boot struck a puddle hard enough to soak his trouser leg. He made an offended noise without slowing.","\"I shall submit the cleaning bill,\" he said.","\"To the emergency committee. It protects everything.\"","They crossed the market, turned behind a delivery cart, and reached June's car. She had left the rear door open. Mara fell into the seat laughing breathlessly, the flower clutched safely inside her coat. The laughter was not amusement. It was relief that Ewan was in a different vehicle, with a doctor he had chosen.","At Mercy House, Noor confirmed that he had no active connection to the company's system. Elena was also disconnected and safe from that route of control. The hospital team checked the equipment they actually used for care rather than throwing ordinary treatment away in fear. Separate practical supply arrangements were made where needed.","Mara went to Elena's room. Her mother was awake, holding one of the new pencils. She listened to the short version and set the pencil down.","\"You can't pull every person out of every chair by yourself,\" she said.","\"I know.\"","\"Then you'll need people who can find the chairs.\"","The sentence followed Mara back into the hall. June had already begun a list of facilities. Rowan was calling contacts who could locate them. Ivo wanted the old maintenance maps. Cassian, without an official pass, was still the person people looked towards when a plan needed to become a set of tasks.","The city's public screens changed just before midnight. Selene Ardent appeared beside the emergency commissioner. Behind them stood a new emblem: COMMONWEALTH OF MEMORY.","Mara watched the first line of the announcement and felt the room grow cold."],"questions":[{"type":"inference","stem":"Why does Ivo decline
